# Handoff

Keep the human section under 15 lines: what changed last, what is next, the exact commands that work, and known issues. Interactive sessions rewrite it when closing a task (see the `close-task` skill). The unattended controller writes its own generated handoff into the run directory and never edits this file.

  - **Quiet-landing panic root-caused: the test was wrong, not the product.** Probe `Q9_PROBE` (debug, 1,200 commits, a commit made outside, `limit` 1,200, quiet rescan) failed 3 of 3: the landing installed a fresh read (outside commit on top, `installed` bumped) of 500 rows, next page at 500. Every read is one page of at most 500 (`worker.rs:922` clamps to `MAX_HISTORY_PAGE`; core `history.rs:64` refuses more); the product sets `limit` only to 500, and more rows come only through Older, which makes History deep and not followed. Not a WS3 regression; no rebuild; `QA-86.md` keeps `gitturtle-a50eae8` (note added). `0a1def0a` pages Older to 5,000 rows in the benchmark; release, 2 runs under the quiet hold (`.local/bench-quiet-2026-11-02/`): 160 of 160 landings drew no frame, p50 1.27 ms (500 followed) and 1.17 ms (5,000 paged); recorded in `adeac057` (`docs/benchmarks/2026-10-29-columns-in-frame.md`, "Quiet landing, 2026-11-02"). Fast gate: `.local/gate-QL-0a1def0a.log`.

    - **Lessons:** an `amend!`/`fixup!` that autosquash moves before a later commit can conflict on adjacent text: keep it as its own commit (a custom `GIT_SEQUENCE_EDITOR`) or resolve by phrase, then confirm the tree equals the agent's tip; PC3 and NT4 hit the 200-turn limit again; start a background gate with the tool's background mode, not `&`, so it notifies.

  - **F security review: pass with fixes.**
    - Low, same root cause as above: a rebuild moves the cursor to a different action and keeps it there. Worst case: at a narrow width a file becomes conflicted, "Discard changes…" is renamed, the cursor falls to Stage, and Enter runs `git add` on the conflicted path, marking it resolved. The commit menu falls to Cherry-pick… when Amend/Undo disappear.
    - Informational: the patch note wrongly lists Editor/Textarea as `ContextMenuExt` callers (they use the native input menu, and the app uses both); `working_selection.rs:313-317` doc is stale.
    - No disabled item can run, and no new lifetimes.
    - **Decision (coordinator, a routine safety call):** a rebuild never moves the cursor to another action. It rests on nothing and remembers the original until that item can act again; a menu key or the pointer ends the remembering. This replaces F.md's "nearest available item" fallback.

After those, item 9's approved taste items, one commit each with native evidence. **Background on the font, kept for reference:** Every `FontWeight` in the interface renders Regular on Linux (count chips, selected labels, SemiBold headings): a throwaway test resolved `.SystemUIFont` at 400/500/600/700 to one face, Adwaita Sans Regular, because gpui's Linux `.SystemUIFont` is `IBM Plex Sans` (`gpui-pre-linux` `platform.rs:150`), not installed, and `TextSystem::resolve_font` (`gpui-pre` `text_system.rs:147`) walks its fallback stack at weight 400; variable desktop fonts (Adwaita Sans, Cantarell) load as one face, so no installed family fixes it. Owner decision, 2026-10-27: do what is best for Linux and the project, legally. Plan: embed IBM Plex Sans, unmodified static TTFs (Regular, Medium, SemiBold, Bold; Italic if the UI draws italics) from IBM's official `IBM/plex` release with recorded sha256, registered at startup beside `desktop_text::code_font_faces` on Linux only, so `.SystemUIFont` (theme, kit and `gpui-base` input elements alike) resolves to real weights without a toolkit patch. License: SIL OFL 1.1 with Reserved Font Name "Plex", so no subsetting or renaming; follow DejaVu's pattern (`assets/fonts/dejavu-sans-mono/README.md`, `docs/licenses/assets/`, the package notices). Inter (closest to today's Adwaita look) would need a gpui patch for the hard-coded name. Then a Linux test that 400/500/600/700 resolve to distinct faces, DESIGN.md's UI line ("Linux uses its available system sans family") and `docs/linux.md`, and native evidence: every screen changes and several breakpoints are measured from the interface font (History toolbar, review controls, header), so check narrow windows too; show the owner before/after frames. Item 4 taste items for the owner are at the end of the run's `findings.md` (light-theme disabled ~2:1, Lumon's and the identity button's disabled primary tint, the palette dialog not raised, light menus equal to the canvas, edges fitted to exactly 1.3). **Then (8):** against `origin/main` `a755d60`, still open are `switch-focus-ring-and-hover`, `switch-checked-knob-and-disabled-label`, `settings-focus-reveal-and-steady-switch`, `markdown-diff-fence-colours`, `review-file-preparation-lifecycle`, `narrow-large-text-compact-chrome`, `copy-actions-say-copied`, `review-arrow-buttons-hover`, `menu-checked-items-accessible` and `builtin-light-hover-lift`; the other ten landed. **(9) candidates noticed:** the status bar in Working Changes still shows History's file-list figures ("2 changed files · file list 2.0 ms" with three working changes). Cause traced 2026-10-27: `Output::WorkingPreview` (`crates/app/src/main.rs:1137`) replaces `self.files` with the one previewed file, so `Output::Preview` writes "1 changed files" (plural also wrong at `main.rs:1220`, `:1247`, `revision_inspection.rs:315`); until a preview arrives the bar keeps History's text. The same line carries operation failures (`workspace.rs:232`), so set the working summary at event time (working preview and status refresh in Changes), not at render time. QA traps: silence notifications while capturing frames (`omarchy-shell notifications setDnd on`, then `off`; this session's own notifications landed in frames); a sweep ends on its last theme, so restore with `OMARCHY_THEME_SKIP_BACKGROUND=1 omarchy theme set "Tokyo Night"` and `omarchy-shell -q background set ~/.local/state/omarchy/current/theme/backgrounds/0-winding-road.jpg`; this host has no privacy templates, and the owner approved committing demo-fixture frames after a full-resolution visual check (2026-10-27); with an empty `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `fc-match` stops seeing `~/.config/fontconfig`, so link it into the QA config; `wtype -M ctrl comma` reaches the app as Ctrl+O (the folder picker), so open Settings with its gear; `OMARCHY_THEME_SKIP_BACKGROUND=1 omarchy theme set` avoids intros but records wrong per-theme wallpapers, so remove `~/.local/state/omarchy/theme-backgrounds/` and restore the `current/background` link afterwards. Owner decision, 2026-10-26: every Omarchy feature is finished before distribution (phase 4) or updates start. Owner decision: update checks for the planned gitturtle.com macOS downloads come later, after the app itself is done. Harness notes: Hyprland 0.56 takes Lua dispatchers (`hyprctl dispatch 'hl.dsp.focus({ window = "class:com.gitturtle.GitTurtle" })'`); `wtype -M logo` fires the wrong binding and can leave fcitx5 ignoring dispatched keys until `uwsm-app -- fcitx5 -r -d --disable notificationitem` restarts it; the orca computer-use backend cannot load AT-SPI from mise's Python; do not mix `wtype` and `hl.dsp.send_key_state` in one sequence (dispatched keys then act as other keys), or run `hyprctl switchxkblayout apple-mtp-keyboard 0` after `wtype` to restore the physical keymap first; `wtype -M ctrl comma` reaches the app as Ctrl+O (the folder picker), so open Settings with its gear; a scratch `zwlr_virtual_pointer_v1` client drives drags.

It landed as #88. `code-font-cold-launch` (measurement only): the setting adds 7 ms to the first window at p50 with a warm fontconfig cache and 23 to 29 ms with a cold one. With no fontconfig cache the family lookup outlives its 500 ms bound and the app never retries, so the family arrives only with the window-activation refresh, about 0.8 s after launch, and in 2 of 24 launches not at all ([record](../benchmarks/2026-09-29-code-font-cold-launch.md), [validation entry](../validation.md#september-29-desktop-code-font-at-a-cold-launch)); reported for its own task. Native keyboard QA on this host needs Hyprland focus to reach the app: `input:follow_mouse` is 1, so move the pointer onto the window before a focus dispatch, and check `hyprctl activewindow` before any key.

- Open findings:
  - **VoiceOver (needs a Mac):** AccessKit's macOS adapter announces a new live node only from its value, so the app's label-only polite status nodes, the command palette's among them, are probably silent under VoiceOver. #78 sets both; the others need `.aria_value` and a check on a Mac.
  - **Stale frames (done 2026-09-29 on the Ubuntu GNOME 50 host):** `button-focus-ring-recapture` retook 64 frames: the 49 named in the September 27 entry, plus 15 more found by the sweep. It also re-pointed the draw-cost focus finder ([validation entry](../validation.md#september-29-frames-retaken-under-the-solid-focus-ring)). New finding there: a Your themes row action above the import highlight loses its ring's bottom edge, because the next row's fill paints over it. A row flush against the status bar loses it to the page viewport. The owner keeps `DESIGN.md`'s whole-ring promise (2026-09-29): fix the product, never weaken the text.
  - **That host (Ubuntu 26.04, GNOME 50, RTX 3090):** XWayland is `:0` and routes XTest through the RemoteDesktop portal, whose "Allow Remote Interaction" prompt swallowed keys (the owner denied it), so QA input there goes through `org.gnome.Mutter.RemoteDesktop` (the local `.local/recapture-2026-09-29/tools/mutter_input.py`), never XTest. The FileChooser is Nautilus 50. The session log, drivers and briefs are in `.local/recapture-2026-09-29/`, the runs are in `/tmp/gitturtle-evidence/runs/recapture/` and the privacy templates are in `.local/privacy/templates`.
  - **Theme editor:** `max_height` ignores the kit's window paddings under untiled Linux client decorations, where the panel would overrun by about 20 px, and a wrapped save error makes the footer taller. Neither occurs on X11 or macOS. At 18 pt the 16 px inset puts the panel's edge across the status-bar hints, and `DESIGN.md` does not say how the status bar looks under a modal. Owner taste, from the #83 review: a graph-lane warning marks Text, whose only fix would invert the theme, and the glyph's accessible name, "‹label› has a readability warning", may read better as "‹label› affects a readability warning".

Child sessions drop environment variables that could override that selection. The owner's `~/.claude/settings.json` sets `modelSettings.claude-opus-5-5.effortLevel` to `xhigh`, which the explicit `--effort` overrides. An interactive session is the evidence owner: read `status`, capture base and candidate with the `native-qa` agent (display `:0`; ask the owner first, including about `idle-delay`), `attest` and `resume`, following the runbook's [native attestation procedure](README.md#native-and-external-attestations) (committing evidence advances `accepted_head` and costs one rebuild per task, hence `--max-attempts 3`). Keep `scripts/agent_loop/` unchanged while a run is saved: a run pins its controller by digest. **Landing:** accepted commits accumulate in the run's private `accepted` clone. Move each into its own `claude/<topic>` branch and PR, and merge on green CI as before; resume the saved run afterwards rather than starting the queue again. Host notes: give every verifier and revert experiment a target of its own, and build each candidate in a fresh one. Full-window project hub frames do not pass the local privacy scan; commit crops of the action panel. GNOME's `idle-delay` is 900. Whether Omarchy's portal serves GNOME's keys through `xdg-desktop-portal-gtk` is unchecked. Other sessions merge to `main` too (#103, #105), so merge `main` into a branch just before its last CI run. Open for the owner, unchanged: text scales below about 0.875 and the CI cache budget (7.47 of 10 GB).     - Settings: a Switch row keeps the height of its tallest description, so neither the Switch nor the rows below move (app-only).

    - Menus: any item built with `.checked(…)` is a checkable item. Copy feedback without a button (a menu item or shortcut) is a polite announcement only.
    - The repository tab strip is at least the tab height plus twice the ring room, so it grows only at the smallest text sizes.
    - Worktree rows: rows stand the ring room plus 2 px apart, and a selected row keeps the neutral border with the selected fill, so an accent outline always means focus.
    - Reflog: the selected entry gets `.selected`, the content gets the always-visible scrollbar of the other review dialogs, and the changed-file list shows whole rows with a scrollbar.
    - Tab onto a scrolled-out row scrolls the least amount to the nearest edge plus the ring room, through every scrolling ancestor, without animation; pointer clicks do not scroll.
    - LFS: a new original `file-download.svg` (a page with a down arrow), since `pull.svg` and `fetch.svg` mean Pull and Fetch.
    - Under a modal the status bar stays dimmed and inert, and the theme editor's panel ends at least 16 px above it inside the client-decoration paddings, its body giving up room when the footer grows.

- **Security sweep (2026-10-25, interactive, Linux AArch64 Omarchy host): landed.** Dependabot #193 (upload-artifact 7.0.2, download-artifact 8.0.2); #195 took the October security releases (rustls, webpki roots, tokio, quinn, zip 9.0.5, ufbx 0.11.5, roxmltree 0.21.2, tree-sitter-md 0.5.4) and closed the ufbx wrapper's notice record; #196 refuses Git LFS before 3.8.1 for preview downloads (CVE-2026-105802, CVE-2026-105803); #197 adds the daily `advisories.yml` cargo-deny check, which is not required, plus a seven-day Dependabot cooldown that also covers the composite setup action. The record is the October 25 update in [the build audit](../security-build-audit.md).

The app could warn before clone, checkout or pull when the installed Git LFS predates 3.8.1, since those operations use the user's own filters; that is a product decision. `mac` 0.1.1 is now the only Linux notice gap. zizmor 1.31.1 still suggests `$/` self-repository syntax in nine places; check that actionlint accepts it first. icu_normalizer 2.4.0 and json5 1.3.4 were left for a later look. - Owner items: the Tailscale review is the owner's (planned for 2026-09-28). The owner decided on 2026-09-25 not to purge the pre-scrub screenshots from history.
- Evidence method that held, now tracked as `python3 scripts/native_qa/qa.py` ([README](../../scripts/native_qa/README.md)): `launch` runs the app under XWayland (`DISPLAY=:1`, `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` unset, `GPUI_X11_SCALE_FACTOR=1`) with a fresh absolute `HOME`/`XDG_*`, an empty run directory, a `GitTurtle QA <qa@example.invalid>` `.gitconfig` and fixtures under `/tmp/gitturtle-evidence/`, finds its window by `_NET_WM_PID` and stops it with SIGTERM only; `compare` (masked base-versus-candidate frames), `privacy scan`, `identity` (sha256 and `--build-info`; a base built into the candidate's target directory once made the candidate build a no-op) and `display-check` (`pgrep` and `date -u`, run by the coordinator before each handover) cover the rest. On 2026-09-25 it reproduced the committed `button-states/midnight-1000x680-rows-button-hover.png` byte for byte, and a Midnight History frame matched its reference apart from the timing digits. The evidence a visible change needs, in a light and a full tier, is in [visual evidence tiers](../../.agents/skills/gitturtle-native-qa/references/visual-evidence.md). Privacy templates stay local (the git-ignored `evidence-followups-5899e2a/b2/tools/privacy/tpl` of the claude-code-support worktree), as do the old one-off drivers. Performance claims follow the [performance skill](../../.agents/skills/gitturtle-performance/SKILL.md): release builds pinned to the P-cores against a same-session base designated in writing. The FileChooser portal dialogs are Wayland clients: capture them with Mutter ScreenCast `RecordArea`, and type into them through Mutter RemoteDesktop only while a widget inside the dialog has focus. On 2026-09-27 unguarded keys (16:33 to 16:42 UTC) went to a focus that was never verified. For AT-SPI, set `org.a11y.Status IsEnabled` true for one launch, then restore it and read it back; with it off the app never registers.
- Commands: `python3 scripts/gate.py fast` (or `full`); `cargo test --locked --workspace`; `umask 022 && python3 -m unittest discover -s scripts/agent_loop -t scripts -p 'test_*.py'`; `python3 scripts/check-agent-guidance.py`.
- Known issues: a new macOS-only GPUI view-test flake most likely has one of #48's two causes: a reply from a real `SerialExecutor` thread that `run_until_parked` does not wait for (drain it with the test-only `SerialExecutor::drain()`), or a dialog's wall-clock animation (open it with reduced motion). Keep other builds off the host while a gate runs. The `gh` HTTPS token lacks the `workflow` scope, so a push that changes `.github/workflows/` is refused: push over SSH (`git push git@github.com:FernandoX7/GitTurtle.git <branch>`). In the agent-loop controller, a `blocked` status does not keep a task out of selection; hold a task by setting `attempts` to `max_attempts` and restore it later. A failed gate replaces the coordinator note and starts the next attempt at once. Background agents can hang on an `rm` with a glob, so delete with Python. Subagents cannot write report files, so they return findings as text, and a `SendMessage` to a busy background subagent is read only when its turn ends, so put every decision in the brief. Two traps from 2026-09-28 on the Pop!_OS host: another window took X focus during a QA run (17:02 to 17:05 UTC), so QA keys now go through the drivers' focus-checked sender; and two worktrees built into one `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` gave different commits an identical binary, so give each candidate its own target directory and check `--build-info`.
